<meta>The author continues discussing the cultural and theological significance of the serpent, transitioning from its association with prophets to its role as a sacred symbol in various global traditions and its typological connection to Christ.</meta>

...and became great prophets because of it. This "incantation by the ears" is elegantly applied by the Church Fathers in their writings to the preachers of the Gospel and to our Savior himself. Clement of Alexandria, in *The Tutor*, Book 5 <note>original: *Clemens in pædagog. V.*</note>, calls him the "Enchanter" <note>original: *Επωδός* (Epōdos)</note>, as the learned Spanheim observes; and Saint Chrysostom often uses the same expression.

All these factors together provide good reasons—omitting several more for the sake of brevity—for the extraordinary veneration paid to this creature since ancient times. Our oldest pagan writer, Sanchoniathon, says the Phoenicians called it the "good spirit" <note>original: *agathodæmon*</note> or the good angel. Epies the Phoenician, quoted in Eusebius, declares it a most divine animal. Maximus of Tyre, whom I quoted earlier, writes that the serpent was the great symbol of the deity in most nations, even among the Indians. Sigismund, in his *History of Muscovy*, says the same of the Samogitians in the northern parts of that vast empire. Gaguin says this of the Lithuanians in his work *Sarmatia*. Likewise, Scaliger, in his notes on Aristotle’s *History of Animals*, says this regarding the people of Calicut in the East Indies; all travel books about the West Indies say the same. This sufficiently proves that the concept is nearly as old as mankind.

From these ancient ideas arose the strange disposition of the Ophite <note>A Gnostic sect that gave primary importance to the serpent in their theology</note> sect or heresy, who claimed the serpent that tempted Eve was the son of God. Epiphanius, Tertullian, Saint Augustine, and others speak of it. They kept a serpent in a box and worshiped it.

2. We must consider the nature of how symbols are formed. The serpent—insofar as it was cursed by God and was a "composite" figure when hanging on a tree—was symbolic of Christ, according to the understanding of both Jewish and Christian writers.

We have viewed the serpent in a very favorable light to remove our prejudices by showing the high regard in which it is held in natural history; much more could have been added to this. However, such a high view is not strictly necessary for forming symbols. If we should think this a mean and contemptible animal, unworthy of representing such a great idea, I reply that it was one of the methods used by the inventors of symbols and emblems to represent the highest things through what we might consider the lowliest subjects. A beetle, for instance, is the symbol of nothing less than what the pagans call the "soul of the world" <note>original: *anima mundi*</note>. They also represented the greatest good by its opposite. Just as Isaiah, in his prophetic style, calls that most excellent prince, King Hezekiah, by the names of dragon, basilisk, cockatrice, and fiery flying serpent (Isaiah 14:29) <note>Note: The text cites xiv. 26, but the descriptions of Hezekiah as a "fiery flying serpent" are found in Isaiah 14:29</note>. This is understood not as a reference to any wickedness <note>original: pravity</note> in his own character, but in relation to the enemies of God's people, to whom he was like a dragon—a divine avenger against foes and a protector of his own people. Furthermore, consider the serpent as a prophylactic symbol <note>A protective symbol used to ward off disease or evil</note> and the highest of sacred characters, thought to most effectively guard against and drive off all evil power. The method for creating these protective symbols was to take the figure of the thing one wanted to remedy. A most remarkable and relevant instance of this is the famous brazen serpent erected by Moses, which was suspended on a cross-pole like those used for military banners. Those who were bitten by the fiery serpents were ordered to look at this and be healed. Thus, the symbol is clearly meant to excite faith and obedience; these virtues are the true cure, rather than any internal power within the symbolic figure itself (Wisdom of Solomon 16:6-7).

All Jewish and Christian writers agree unanimously that this was a type <note>A person or thing in the Old Testament believed to foreshadow something in the New Testament</note> of the Messiah. Philo is ecstatic about it, supposing that it signifies something extraordinary and future. Rabbi Moses Gerundinensis <note>Better known as Nachmanides</note> writes as follows: "It..."

<page-num>60</page-num>
# A BURY

"It seems to me, concerning this mystery, that it is agreeable to the course of divine law—as with miraculous works—that the mischief should be remedied by something similar to that which caused it." This makes the miracle even more illustrious and divine: that God should direct a snake to cure those bitten by snakes.

Other Rabbis are of the same way of thinking, such as *David Kimchi* in his work *Michlol*, Volume II; *Abarbenel* <note>Isaac Abarbenel (1437–1508), a Jewish statesman and philosopher</note> in his commentary on the passage, folio 305; and *Nachmanides* <note>Also known as Ramban, a leading medieval Jewish scholar</note>. Our Savior applies the *Mosaic serpent* <note>The bronze serpent Moses set on a pole to heal the Israelites (Numbers 21)</note> directly to himself; it is no wonder, then, that the Christian Church Fathers do so as well. "Christ hung on the cross like a serpent," says Saint *Ambrose* <note>original: *Christus veluti serpens in cruce pependit*</note>. *Moebius* treats largely of this resemblance between *Christ* and the serpent in his *Essay on the Bronze Serpent* <note>original: *exercitatio de æneo serpente*</note>, page 63. The serpent was highly honored in this way: just as it had been the instrument for introducing the greatest evil to mankind, it was to the serpent that God’s word was directed when He promised us the greatest good—the Messiah—implied in the words of *Genesis* 3:15: *He shall bruise your head*. The Septuagint <note>The ancient Greek translation of the Old Testament</note> uses the word "he himself" <note>original: *αυτος* (autos)</note> for this.

Another similar case is found in 1 *Samuel* 5. The Ark of God was taken captive by the *Philistines*, and they dared to look into its venerable secrets. Consequently, the nation was struck in their posterior—the organs of generation—which the scripture modestly calls *emerods* <term>hemorrhoids</term>. Moreover, a terrible pestilence killed many, and a plague of mice at harvest-time came upon them and devoured all the fruit of their ground. In order to make an atonement, they sent the Ark away again with golden figures of the hemorrhoids and mice, along with a present of costly jewels as a consecrated ransom <note>original: *λυσγον* (lysgon); likely a variant of *lytron*, meaning satisfaction or ransom</note> or satisfaction to the God of the *Jews*. Here, by the way, we would be blind if we did not see the origin of the *phallus* <note>An image of the male generative organ used as a symbol of fertility</note> among the heathens.

Therefore, to apply this to the seeming difficulty we first noticed: why pay such regard to an animal which the ancestors of mankind had so much reason to detest? Did the devil injure us under the form of a serpent? Then that same figure is the most proper of any to symbolize the remedy—the antidote against the poison by which the devil brought about man's fall. Therefore, naturally, the same figure is used to symbolize the promised Messiah, who is to work man's redemption. Saint *Athanasius*, in Volume II, *Question* 20, does not scruple to make a comparison between the union of the serpent and the devil in the fatal temptation, and the union of the divine and human nature in our blessed Savior. The venomous serpent represents his human nature—sinful and infected by the devil's treachery—for he *was made sin for us, though he was not contaminated himself*. Although he was not venomous, he cures the venom of our nature. I observe that the *Rabbis*, though they saw clearly enough how the *Mosaic serpent* was necessarily applicable to the Messiah, were somewhat fearful of speaking their minds on it for fear of doing wrong by comparing him to an accursed animal. But our Savior himself was not fearful in comparing himself to it; rather, he took it as a very express <term>type</term> <note>A biblical person, event, or object that foreshadows something in the New Testament</note> of his crucifixion and of his being accursed for our sakes (*Deuteronomy* 21:23; *John* 3:14; *Galatians* 3:13)—that is, devoted as a sacrifice or expiation, so that we, being freed from the curse of sin, might obtain the blessing of God. This is how our Christian writers explain the connection between our Savior and the bronze serpent in the wilderness—*Bede* in particular, in his commentary on *John* 3. Here we see the nature of types, where a man who undergoes the curse and punishment of the law becomes, in reality, a type of the Messiah. A serpent, which represents the principle of evil, functions in the same way (2 *Corinthians* 5:21). Assuredly *Moses*, guided by the Holy Spirit, intended it to refer to
->Christ's<-

Christ’s crucifixion. A fit emblem of his divinity, through that remarkable quality of their throwing off old age with their skin and returning to youth again. For so the ancients thought:

> *Old age is cast off by snakes along with their thin skin.* <note>original Latin: *Anguibus exuitur tenui cum pelle vetustas.*</note> — Tibullus.

A fit emblem of his resurrection from the dead, and of returning to an immortal life.

No wonder then, from such reasons as these, and others as obvious, the ancients concluded this to be the most divine of all animals. They thought it the most appropriate symbol of the <term>Second Mind</term> <note>original Greek: *Noûs heteros*</note> of <term>Plato</term>, whom they affirmed to be the creator of the world. I do not know whether this notion of theirs did not further contribute to it: they thought these animals brought forth offspring by the mouth. They also have no limbs or members for action, but exert their mighty power by the mouth only; whence <term>Horus Apollo</term> <note>An author of a treatise on Egyptian hieroglyphics</note> says, “a serpent is the symbol of the mouth.” This well represents the all-creating WORD, which <term>Suidas</term> speaks of from <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure associated with wisdom and writing</note>, as being all-perfect, fruitful, the workman, and creator of the world.

## CHAPTER XII.

*The second sort of temples, called **Dracontia** <note>Serpent temples</note>, like that of **Avebury** <note>original: "Abury"</note>, were built frequently in ancient times. Their traces are pursued here. Part of the history of **Phut**, the third son of **Ham** <note>original: "Cham"</note>. A genealogy of the most ancient sacred and heathen families. Phut had a fleet of ships upon the Mediterranean. He was the **Typhon**, **Typhis**, or **Python** of antiquity, called **Apollo Pythius** after death. He was a builder of these serpentine temples and resembled the Emperor **Augustus** in his facial features. He erected the first patriarchal temple at **Delphi** <note>original: "Delphos"</note>, a Dracontium. **Parnassus** was originally **Larnassus**, which is none other than our **Hakpen** at Avebury. The Sabbath was originally observed there. **Aeas**, a son of Phut, built the Dracontium at **Colchis**. **Perseus**, another of his sons, bore the sacred hierogram—the circle, snake, and wings—on his shield, which is the origin of the **Medusa’s** head.*

**Z**oroaster the Magus (as cited in *Eusebius, Preparation for the Gospel, II. 7*), Plato, Porphyry, and others of the ancient philosophers, define God to be everywhere and nowhere; He who fills all space and is contained in none; “from whom came all things that are, and which are not yet; eternal, immutable, omnipresent, incomprehensible, immaterial, without parts, beginning, or end.” If we put this definition into a geometrical figure in order to form a symbol, we

# AVEBURY <note>original: "ABURY"</note>

I cannot possibly do it better than by describing the circle. A circle, in hieroglyphics, means "divine." Particularly, as it is the most perfect and comprehensive of all geometrical figures, the ancients designed it as the symbol of the first and supreme being—the one whose resemblance we cannot find, whose center is everywhere, and whose circumference is nowhere. As *Abenephi* the Arabian and others assert, it well pictured the divine nature of God. Therefore, this figure of the serpent and circle in their doctrine aptly represents the divine Creator, or the Creator who descended from the Supreme. For though the Deity was the author of all things, the SON or WORD of the Supreme was more immediately the architect of the universe.

This we find to be exactly consistent with scriptural doctrine. It seems very evident to me that the most important divine truths accepted in the Christian church were imparted to the first race of mankind—the patriarchal church—for these two are in reality the same.

We learn repeatedly from *Sanchoniathon*, *Porphyry*, and other ancient authors quoted by *Eusebius* in his *Preparation for the Gospel* <note>original Latin: *Praeparatio Evangelica*</note>, that the first sages of the world had just and true notions of the nature of the Deity, matching those of the Christians. In their hieroglyphic way of writing, they represented the Deity and His mysterious nature by the sacred figure of the circle, snake, and wings. Among these, the circle meant the fountain of all being: the invisible Supreme, who had no name. The serpent symbolized the Son, or the first divine emanation from the Supreme. This they called by the name of *Ptah* <note>original: *Ptha*</note>, which is derived from the Hebrew, meaning the WORD. The wings symbolized that divine person or emanation from the former, commonly called the "Soul of the World" <note>original Latin: *anima mundi*</note>, but the Egyptians called him KNEPH, which in Hebrew signifies "winged."

Thus, the old authors who speak of these things are to be understood, even though they are confused and did not rightly grasp the core of the matter. This hieroglyphic figure, as a whole, was called *Knephtha*.

This knowledge of the nature of the Deity—the most valuable treasure <note>original: *depositum*</note> that could be shared with mortals—was eventually perverted into idolatry. Therefore, Almighty God stopped revealing Himself further on that subject in an explicit manner until the "fullness of time" arrived with the Christian dispensation. However, those people who kept themselves from idolatry—among whom I include our Druids—retained the knowledge that had already been shared. This sacred figure of the winged <note>original: *alate*</note> and serpent-bearing <note>original: *serpentiferous*</note> circle was, as it were, a seal which they stamped upon their most lasting monuments: their temples. I have no doubt that they somewhat improved their notions of it through reasoning, in the manner I shall discuss in Chapter XV.

<margin>Avebury is not the only temple in Britain formed on this design of the circle and serpent. I saw another at Shap in Westmorland when I traveled through the area in 1725 with Mr. Roger Gale. However, I had no opportunity to examine it closely.</margin>

There is another, I believe, at Callanish <note>original: *Classerness*</note>, a village on the Isle of Lewis, between Scotland and Ireland. I took a drawing of it from Mr. Lhuyd’s <note>original: *Lwydd*</note> travels; but he was a very poor artist, and having no knowledge of the purpose of the site, he made the representation even worse. The circle to which it belongs is 20 cubits <note>A cubit is an ancient unit of length, roughly 18 inches or 45 centimeters</note> in diameter. There is a central obelisk. A part of the snake remains extending from it, which he calls an "avenue." He did not discern its curve, any more than he did the curve of the Kennet Avenue, which he also drew in the same collection as a straight line. It seems to me...

<summary>Stukeley argues that the circle-and-serpent motif in ancient architecture represents the Trinity and the Divine Word, a truth shared between the early Patriarchs and the Druids. He identifies other "serpent temples" in Britain, including Shap and Callanish, suggesting they follow the same symbolic design as Avebury.</summary>

-># DESCRIBED.<-

<meta>catchword: Phut,</meta>

...informed me that the circle was double, or two concentric. I will print it in the next volume.

There is no doubt that there are more of these in the British Isles. In this chapter, I propose to present my ideas concerning those in the eastern parts of the world, of which there are many traces in ancient writings, while avoiding wordiness <note>original: "prolixity"</note> as much as possible.

The practice of building these serpentine temples was used by the patriarchs, perhaps near the beginning of the world. I have some proof that they are older than the Great Flood <note>original: "the flood"</note>, but I will not insist on that for now. The first person I will mention in this regard is Phut, a brother of *Canaan* and son of *Ham* <note>original: "Cham"</note>. Phut was a person of much greater importance in antiquity than is commonly thought. But if we wish to know anything of the specific records of this man, or of his relatives and contemporaries <note>original: "coevals"</note>, we have nothing left but pagan <note>original: "heathen"</note> stories.

Although the *Phoenicians*, our Druids, and the *Egyptians* were the first to use alphabetic writing, none of these nations have passed down any records of themselves to us. For what little knowledge we have of them, aside from their monuments, we are entirely indebted to the Greeks, who received these arts from them. They successfully improved art, science, sculpture, and writing, allowing them to pass down most of the ancient history we know outside of the Bible. However, they faced the misfortune of developing the symbolic method of writing—which they learned from the *Phoenicians* and *Egyptians*—to such an extreme degree that it produced what we call by the general name of "mythology." It was only very late that they began to write true history. Consequently, the entire ancient history of the nations they describe is wrapped in this confusing mythology.

Yet we would be very much to blame if we completely ignored it. It is all we have of non-biblical <note>original: "prophane"</note> antiquity. It is more admirable for us to try to untangle it from its symbolic mysteries and discover the plain truth. Those who have been most successful in this find much agreement between mythology and Biblical history, where they overlap.

It is chiefly from this mythology that I can attempt to discuss these great works I have been describing further. I will try to do so with as much brevity and clarity <note>original: "perspicuity"</note> as possible, as is appropriate for such topics. Yet I do not despair of finding a good deal of true history. I cannot guarantee everything, and the reader must be quite open-minded <note>original: "a great deal of candour is necessary"</note> if they wish to find either pleasure or instruction in this. Indeed, as a predecessor in these kinds of inquiries, Dr. Dickinson, says in *The Phoenician Delphi* <note>original: "Delph. Phœnic."</note>, "if we look over Greek mythology with proper wisdom, we shall easily discover many traces <note>original: "footsteps"</note> of true religion."

"A fable is a constructed story consisting of the marvelous, and a philosopher, in a way, is a lover of such things," says the great philosopher <note>referring to Aristotle</note> in *Metaphysics, Book 1, Chapter 2* <note>original: "Metaphys. I. 2."</note>.

There are vast treasures of ancient knowledge in mythology, especially concerning both sacred and civil history. It is all that remains of pagan history from the most ancient times, and it is worth our while to clear away the debris <note>original: "rubbish"</note> and pick out the useful parts. The scholarly works of *Bochart*, *Selden*, *Marsham*, *Huetius*, *Gale*, *Cumberland*, *Banier*, and many others show us its usefulness. We must forgive them if, in some matters, they have overstepped the "golden mean" <note>the ideal middle ground between two extremes</note>; we ourselves will be content to stray somewhat alongside those great names.

He defends the use of mythology as a valid, though difficult, source for understanding the ancient world.</summary>

<page-num>64</page-num>

# AVEBURY

Phut, son of Ham <note>original: "Cham"</note>, was a person of eminence, though he is not noticed as much as he deserves. I believe it is very relevant to our purpose to recount some part of his history. He is the Apollo mentioned by Sanchoniathon <note>An ancient Phoenician author</note> as the son of Cronus (who is Ham), as is demonstrated beyond doubt by Bishop Cumberland in his posthumous works. He is said to have been born in Perea, that is, the country toward the Euphrates. He was Ham’s third son, as is also recorded by Moses. From the name Phut, he was called Python by a slight transposition natural when pronouncing a difficult name; and, by a similar transposition, he was called Typhon.

*Apollo Pythius* was the son of Ammon (that is, Ham), says Lucius Ampelius in his *Book of Memorials* <note>original: "in libro memoriali"</note>. Plutarch, in *On Isis and Osiris* <note>original: "de Isid. & Osir."</note>, writes that Typhon was the brother of Osiris, who was undoubtedly Mizraim <note>original: "Misraim"</note>, the son of Ham. Diodorus Siculus says the same.

To make the study of antiquity easier, I present the reader here with a genealogical table of the great figures we are about to discuss. I could provide the evidence proving each specific descent in a strictly genealogical <note>original: "heraldical"</note> manner, but that would take up too much of our time now.

# ABURY

<term>Phut</term> <note>original: Phut; the third son of Ham, according to Genesis 10:6</note> was the most celebrated navigator of ancient times. He built a fleet of ships and began to transport colonies into the countries surrounding the <term>Mediterranean</term> Sea. <term>Strabo</term> in book IX tells us his history based on the writings of <term>Ephorus</term>, a very ancient historian. He says <term>Phut</term> (or <term>Apollo</term>) traveled the earth and came to the primitive inhabitants of *Parnassus*. His mission was to bring civilization and social manners to men, teaching them to use grain for their food.

<term>Pindar</term> writes of him:

->He traveled over earth and sea, setting watchtowers on hilltops,<-
->among the nations, consecrating temples, and building groves.<-

<term>Lycophron</term> mentions <term>Typhon</term>'s watchtowers in *Arimis*, which is probably the *Percea* of *Sanchoniathon* <note>a Phoenician author</note>, the eastern part of *Syria*. This is where *Homer* says the lair <note>original: εων (eon), likely referring to the Greek *eunē*, or "bed"</note> of <term>Typhon</term> was, in a field full of oaks. It is not unusual for <term>Apollo</term> to be represented as a military captain (see *Hyginus, Fables 140*). And he truly was a leader of a vast colony of his people into *Egypt*, which was then occupied by his elder brother <term>Mizraim</term> <note>original: Misraim</note>. I will say more of this later. <term>Seneca</term> speaks of him in the play *Medea*:

->Tiphys <note>The pilot of the Argo, here identified with Phut</note> dared to spread sails across the vast sea,<-
->and to write new laws for the winds.<-

Again:

->Tiphys, the foremost tamer of the deep.<-

In <term>Jeremiah 46:9</term>, the <term>Libyans</term> of *Africa* are called <term>Phut</term> in the original Hebrew. The <term>Lydians</term> mentioned there are the people or descendants of <term>Lud</term>, who is <term>Thoth</term>, his brother.

<term>Apollodorus (I. 4)</term> writes that <term>Helios</term> <note>original: Elios</note>, our <term>Phut</term>, married <term>Rhode</term>, the daughter of <term>Neptune</term>. Neptune was actually <term>Tarshish</term>, the son of <term>Javan</term>, who was the son of <term>Japheth</term>. From her name, he called the celebrated island *Rhodes*. To his honor, later generations erected the most magnificent bronze statue that ever existed in the world, in any metal or material; it was seventy cubits high, and because of its size, all great statues have since been called <term>Colossuses</term>. The <term>Argonauts</term> in the works of <term>Apollonius (Book I)</term> sacrifice to <term>Apollo</term> as the patron of navigation. In <term>Artemidorus (Oniro II. 35)</term>, he is called <term>Apollo Delphinius</term>; that author says this title refers to long voyages. <term>Pausanias</term> gives him the same surname in his writings on *Boeotia*. Because of this, I believe the dolphin, which is his symbol <note>original: cognizance</note>, was placed in the heavens as a constellation.

In facial appearance, he looked like the emperor <term>Augustus</term>. I have several <term>Rhodian</term> coins in silver and bronze of different sizes, in all of which he is pictured. We need not be hesitant in thinking these are good resemblances. The <term>Telchines</term> <note>mythical metalworkers</note>, inhabitants of *Rhodes*, are said to be the first makers of statues. Today, we can have the satisfaction of seeing many representations of him in the coins, busts, and images of <term>Augustus</term>—particularly the famous statue of <term>Apollo</term> in the <term>Vatican</term> garden at *Rome*, which was modeled after the emperor's face. Therefore, we may reasonably accept it as the heroic portrait of <term>Phut</term>.

The scholar *Bochart* thinks he first settled his home and family at *Delos*, and that the story of his being born there came from that fact. I have an ancient bronze coin featuring the heroic portrait of his mother, <term>Latona</term>. Her head is on the front with the inscription "The Holy Senate" <note>original: ΙΕΡΑ CYNΚΑΕΙΤΟC (Hiera Synklētos)</note>. On the back, the goddess is shown sitting, holding a ceremonial spear <note>original: *hasta pura*, a spear without an iron head used as a symbol of divinity or honor</note> diagonally in her right hand, with the inscription "Of the People of Tripolis" <note>original: ΔΗΤΩ ΤΡΙΠΟΛΕΙΤΩΝ (Lētō Tripoleitōn)</note>.

<summary>This page argues that the biblical Phut was the historical origin of the Greek god Apollo, identifying him as an ancient navigator and civilizer who settled Rhodes and influenced Egypt. The author uses classical literature, biblical geography, and ancient coins to prove that Phut's physical likeness is preserved in statues of Augustus Caesar.</summary>

# DESCRIBED.

In this island of *Delos*, he had a most magnificent temple built to him in later ages, when idolatry began. The noble remains of it can still be seen there. Because of his great fame and exploits, future generations consecrated him, calling him the son of *Jupiter*—meaning *Jupiter Ammon*—or more properly, the son of *Saturn*.

But in no place was *Phut* <note>Phut was the third son of Ham and grandson of Noah; the author identifies him here with the Greek god Apollo.</note> more famous than in *Phocis*. He settled the country around Mount *Parnassus*, where he built—as I understand it—a great serpentine temple at the foot of that mountain, near the city of *Delphi* <note>original: "Delphos"</note>. This temple was likely similar to our own at *Avebury* <note>original: "Abury"</note>. I gather this from the *Greek* reports of the serpent *Python*, a creature of immense size born from the slime left on the earth by the Great Flood. *Apollo* overcame this serpent here and established the annual games called the *Pythian Games*, clearly named after himself. These were the first and most ancient games we hear of in *Greece*.

If we swap the locations—*Avebury* for *Parnassus*—we find both the natural and chronological history of the place: a vast temple in the form of a serpent, made from stones left on the surface of the earth after the deluge. Even the names match. *Stephanus of Byzantium* <note>A 6th-century Byzantine author of a geographical dictionary.</note> says the name of *Parnassus* was originally *Larnassus*. As the learned *Dickenson* observes in *Delphi Phoenicizing*, the letter "L" is not a root in this word; therefore, the word is actually *Harnassus*. *Har* is a headland or promontory of a hill, and *nahas* is a serpent. This is exactly the same as our *Hakpen* <note>The "serpent's head" hill at Avebury.</note> at *Avebury*. From this, we conclude that the snake-shaped temple extended its huge length along the bottom of *Parnassus* and laid its head upon a promontory, just like ours at *Avebury* sits on *Overton Hill*. This is why *Ovid* describes it not merely poetically, but physically:

-> Covering so many acres with its belly. <note>original: "Tot jugera ventre prementem."</note> <-

***

This was the original patriarchal temple dedicated to the true God, where oracles were originally given by *Themis*, according to *Apollodorus* (Book 1, Chapter 4). I believe the name *Themis* is a later corruption of the *Jewish Thummim* <note>Part of the "Urim and Thummim," objects used by ancient Hebrew priests to determine the divine will.</note>, signifying a divine and true oracle. *Dickenson* asserts (page 104) that this place was originally a site of true worship that in time turned into an idolatrous one. Many temples were built there, one after another, as the previous ones were looted and destroyed.

The report that the mountain was once called *Larnassus* is another argument for the high antiquity of this first serpentine temple built here by *Phut*. it connects us to the patriarchal church and the era immediately following the Great Flood. *Stephanus of Byzantium*, whom I quoted before, says this; the interpreter of *Apollonius* and *Ovid* also claim *Apollo’s* battle with the *Python* happened immediately after the flood. The Greeks pretend the name *Larnassus* comes from *Larnax*—the ark of *Deucalion* <note>The hero of the Greek flood myth, similar to Noah.</note>—which they say landed here. This is typical of the *Greek* habit of claiming all ancient history as their own.

The central obelisk-like stone in some of the circular works here served as the *Qibla* <note>original: "Kebla"; the point toward which people turn to pray.</note>, much like in the southern temple of *Avebury*. In later idolatrous times, this stone was worshipped at *Delphi* as the statue of *Apollo*, as *Clement of Alexandria* writes in his *Miscellanies* <note>original: "Stromata"</note>, Book 1. This continued until art and *Grecian* refinement improved and produced elegant images, like the one in the *Vatican* mentioned before and countless others that still remain.

In *Vaillant’s* book on colony coins (Volume 1, page 242), there is an elegant coin struck at *Caesarea* for the Emperor *Antoninus Pius*. On the reverse side, *Apollo* is standing, leaning on a tripod, and holding an extended snake in his right hand. The learned author is at a loss to explain this, so I may be allowed to offer my opinion: it relates directly to our present subject of serpentine temples.

He uses linguistic roots and archaeological remains to suggest these sites were originally dedicated to the "true God" before being corrupted by Greek mythology and idolatry.</summary>

# AVEBURY <note>original: "A BURY"</note>

It was the practice of the ancient founders of colonies to begin their work by building temples—I mean our ancestral <note>original: "patriarchal"</note> temples, for there were then no others. They also established festivals and religious games, which contributed greatly to polishing and civilizing mankind and giving them a proper understanding and practice of religion, without which it is impossible for any state to survive. Strabo writes very sensibly about this in Book IX, discussing this very location. The *Paeanic* or *Pythian* games are the most ancient ones of which we have any account. Strabo writes quite extensively concerning them.

These great festivals occurred at the four solar entries into the cardinal signs <note>The points when the sun enters Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn—the equinoxes and solstices.</note>, which I suppose have been the times of public sacrifice since the creation of the world. The *Pythian* festival was celebrated on the sixth day of the Athenian month *Thargelion* (the Delphic month *Busius*). This falls between April and May.

But we learn from the scholar <note>original: "scholiast"</note> of Pindar (in the introduction to the *Pythian Odes*) that Apollo established the *Pythia* on the seventh day after he had overcome the serpent *Python*; and that at Delphi <note>original: "Delphos"</note> they sang a hymn called a *Paean* to Apollo every seventh day. The Athenians did the same every seventh day of the moon, which is the source of Hesiod's line:

->The seventh is a holy day<-
<note>original Greek: Ἐβδόμη ἱερὸν ἦμαρ</note>

This was, he says, because Apollo was born on that day.

The learned Gale observes from this, in his *Court of the Gentiles* (p. 150), that this refers to the Sabbath as an ancestral custom that existed before the Jewish institution. Archbishop Usher was of the same opinion before him in his discourse on the Sabbath. Porphyry, in his book concerning the Jews (quoted by Eusebius in *Preparation for the Gospel*, Book I, chapter 9), tells us that the Phoenicians consecrated one day in seven as holy. He says, indeed, that it was in honor of their principal deity *Saturn* (as they called him) and *Israel*. We should not focus on his reasoning any more than Hesiod’s mentioned above, but his testimony regarding a matter of fact carries its proper weight. He intends to prove a custom older than Judaism.

I take all this to be an illustrious proof of the ancestral observation of the Sabbath before the Mosaic law <note>original: "Mosaick dispensation"</note> was given. Their Sabbath was entirely like our Christian one: the greatest festival of all, and deservedly the one most to be regarded as being religion itself, or practical religion.

We cannot easily determine on which day the ancestral Sabbath was kept. Hesiod’s reasoning—being the birthday of Apollo—suggests Sunday; Porphyry’s suggests Saturday. Following the latter, Martial wrote in Book XII, 63:

->*To Saturn*<-
->*Great king of the ancient heavens and the prior world,*<-
->*under whom there was lazy rest, and no labor existed.*<-
<note>original Latin: Antiqui Rex magne poli, mundique prioris, / Sub quo pigra quies, nec labor ullus erat.</note>

But both show evidently the antiquity of the seven-day <note>original: "hebdomadal"</note> division of time, the planetary names of the weekdays, and the original Sabbatical rest. Pausanias, in his *Description of Greece* (Attica), writes that at Megara there was a statue of Apollo carrying the *Decimae* or tithes—another ancestral usage.

The work of Phut <note>original: "Phœu," likely a transcription error for Phut</note> building an enormous serpentine temple was called "killing" or "overcoming" the huge serpent *Python*, who was properly called the "son of the earth."

->— *And you also, greatest Python,*<-
->*the earth then brought forth: to new peoples you were*<-
->*an unknown serpent, a terror. You occupied so much space on the mountain.*<-
<note>original Latin: Et te quoque maxime Python / Tum genuit: populisque novis incognita serpens / Terror eras. Tantum spatii de monte tenebas. From Ovid's Metamorphoses.</note>

<summary>This page argues that the Pythian Games and the seven-day week (Sabbath) were ancestral traditions dating back to the "patriarchal" era before Judaism. The author interprets the mythological slaying of the Python as a metaphor for Phut building a massive serpentine temple.</summary>

<keywords>Sabbath history, Pythian Games, Apollo, Python, Phut, patriarchal religion, seven-day week, Ovid, Strabo, Porphyry</keywords>

### DESCRIBED.

Since public sacrifices, games, hymns, and a Sabbath observance were celebrated there, we have good reason to think all the same were observed by our Druids at Abury <note>original: "Albury"; likely referring to the great stone circle at Avebury</note>, especially considering they were of Phoenician origin.

To conclude this chapter, this work of Phut’s <note>Phut was the third son of Ham and grandson of Noah, often associated in early modern scholarship with the settlement of North Africa and the Mediterranean</note> is mentioned in many places. Some say it was in Mysia; others in Phrygia, or again in Cilicia, Pithecusae, or Boeotia. Strabo writes that it was in Syria, and there seems to have been a serpentine temple on the river Orontes at Antioch, for it was originally called *Typhon* and *Ophis* <note>original: "ΟΦΙΣ"; Greek for "serpent"</note>, as Strabo writes in book 16, and Eustathius notes in his commentary on the *Iliad* (page 262, Basel edition) and on Dionysius. The story tells of Typhon, a huge serpent, slain there by a thunderbolt from Jupiter near a sacred cave called the Nymphaeum.

The meaning of all this seems to be that Phut himself, or his people, built these temples in all these locations. Aeetes <note>original: "Ææas"; the mythological King of Colchis</note>, a son of Phut, built the serpentine temple at Colchis.

Perseus was a son of Demaroon, born in Egypt (according to Eusebius’s *Preparation for the Gospel*, book 2, chapter 1). He was a contemporary of Phut and bore the sacred hierogram <term>hierogram</term><note>a sacred symbol, in this case likely a serpent or dragon emblem</note> on his shield; he probably built some of these *Dracontia* <term>Dracontia</term><note>temples or monuments constructed in the shape of a serpent</note>. From this, the poets created their fable of Medusa’s head, claiming it turned men into snakes. Hesiod, in his description of Hercules’s shield, depicts him thus:

> “As he went, his unbreakable shield rang and tinkled with a loud noise. Two dragons were suspended in a circle, lifting up their heads.”

John Malalas <note>original: "Johannes Malala"; a 6th-century Byzantine chronicler</note> identifies Perseus as the founder of the Magi, who were the patriarchal priests of the East. He calls the previously mentioned river of Antioch the *Dracon* <note>Greek for "Dragon" or "Serpent"</note>.

# DESCRIBED.

The dynasty of the Shepherd Kings <note>original: "pastor kings"; today more commonly known by the Greek term "Hyksos"</note> is what we are chiefly concerned with, as it belongs to the most early ages after the flood. Sir John Marsham has dated them too late. Bishop Usher and Cumberland are much closer to the truth, in my opinion, and I differ from them very little. The last of this dynasty of shepherds is Assis (also known as Archles), our "Egyptian Hercules." They were Canaanites who followed Mizraim <note>original: "Misraim"; the biblical name for the progenitor of the Egyptians</note> into Egypt. At first, they lived very peaceably, but in time the two families quarreled and waged terrible wars against each other for 200 years. The Mizraimites possessed the upper regions of the Nile, while the Canaanites held the lower or marshy part by the Mediterranean Sea, called the Delta. Because of this, the former called them "Titans"—meaning "dirty," "fen-men," or "bog-trotters," as we might say contemptuously of a people who are their actual descendants. The Mizraimites called themselves the *Elohim* <note>original: "Elohim"; a Hebrew term for God or gods</note>, or Gods, claiming to be descendants of Ilus or Ham <note>original: "Cham"</note>. They lived, as it were, in a heavenly region toward Egyptian Ethiopia, where Homer describes the gods holding their festivals. Similarly, the Greeks called those who lived in the high countries *Athamanes*, meaning "heavenly." Mount Olympus was heaven, the home of the gods. This was the standard way of speaking during the heroic ages.

The Canaanites, on the other hand, called themselves *Hyksos* <note>original: "Hycsi"</note>, or Royal Shepherds. The stories of the battles between these two peoples are the oldest legends we have from the ancient poets, specifically the tales of the wars between the Gods and the Titans.

In the chronology of this shepherd dynasty, I differ slightly from the great authors mentioned above. The main reason for this is as follows: they rely on the numbers in Josephus's catalog as they appear in modern copies, but I believe those are incorrect and should be corrected using the works of Africanus, Eusebius, and Syncellus, who copied from Josephus in earlier times. The numbers currently found in Josephus are somewhat too low. Although Africanus, Eusebius, and Syncellus differ from one another as well as from Josephus—a common result of sloppy transcription—they all agree that the numbers should be higher. Furthermore, Josephus himself, twice in the same books, gives a total sum of 393 years, which is more than the sum of the individual parts used by Marsham, Usher, and Cumberland. However, if you take that total sum of 393 years, set it at the date of the Exodus, and count backward, I believe we find its correct historical placement.

By this method, the start of the shepherd dynasty in Egypt—which began with Salatis—must be placed in the year 1860 AM <note>original: "anno mundi"; Latin for "Year of the World," counting from the creation</note> instead of 1920, as Usher and Cumberland suggest. This places it during the reigns of Menes, Mizraim, and Osiris, according to their own chronology. I am confident this is close to the truth, and this is how that dynasty should be positioned in the timeline of history.

->*Manetho's* <note>original: "Manethon"</note> dynasties of Shepherd Kings in Lower Egypt.<-

| Ruler | Julian Period <note>original: "A. P. J."; Anno Periodi Julianae</note> | Year of the World <note>original: "A.M."; Anno Mundi</note> |
|:---|:---|:---|
| *Salatis* began to reign | 2570 | 1860 |
| *Beon* | | 1879 |
| *Apachnas* | | 1923 |
| *Apophis* | | 1959 |
| *Janias Staan* | | 2020 |
| *Assis, Archles, Melqart* <note>original: "Melcartus"</note> | 2781 | 2071 |

Through this, we see the opening scene of the most important events of antiquity, which relate to the world in general and specifically to the island of Great Britain, of which I must provide some account.

In the year of the world 2083, the great patriarch Abraham came out

<meta>The previous page discussed the early history of the "Shepherd Kings" (Hyksos) in Egypt, specifically the reign of the "Egyptian Hercules" (Assis) and the conflict between the Canaanite settlers and the native Mizraimites. It concluded with the phrase "of".</meta>

# ABURY

...of Chaldea into the land of Canaan. This occurred in the 13th year of the reign of our Melcarthus in lower Egypt. About the year 2087 <note>original: "anno mundi"; Year of the World, a dating system starting from the biblical Creation</note>, not 2084 (as Archbishop Usher sets it), Abraham was forced by famine to go down to Egypt—that is, into lower Egypt. Therefore, our Melcarthus is the actual Pharaoh mentioned in Genesis 12, who would have taken Sarah, Abraham's wife, until he learned the truth. Usher, at the year 2084, calls him Apophis; but this is a slip of the pen, and he likely means Janias, the predecessor to Assis, whom he identifies as regent from the year 2081.

Castor the chronographer, as quoted by Syncellus, writes, "that Abraham was well-learned in the knowledge of astronomy, and the other sciences of the Chaldeans." Berosus, the author of the *Chaldean History*, gave him the character of "a just and great man, expert in astronomy." Josephus adds, "that Hecateus had such a high regard for his memory, that he wrote his history." Nicholas of Damascus, a historian, and Trogus both describe him as a king. Alexander Polyhistor relates from Eupolomus, "that Abraham exceeded all men in wisdom; that astronomy was founded by him among the Chaldeans; that he came into Phoenicia and taught the Phoenicians astronomy; that being forced by famine, he went into Egypt, lived in Heliopolis among the priests, and taught them astronomy; yet he did not pretend to be the inventor of the art, but had it delivered to him by succession from Enoch." Artapanus likewise, the historian mentioned by Eusebius in the *Preparation for the Gospel* <note>original: "præp. evang. IX. 4"</note>, speaks of "Abraham going to the king of Egypt and teaching him astronomy, and that after twenty years he returned into Syria." Melo, another ancient non-Christian author, speaks much of Abraham's wisdom. These writers, being entirely unbiased <note>original: "disinterested"</note>, sufficiently show that Egypt learned astronomy from him, and Melcarthus their king in particular.

It seems that at this time, the majority of the world—through ignorance or negligence—did not know the true length of a year, calculating it as only 360 days. But Abraham taught the Egyptians better; for now we can understand that remark in Syncellus, that under Assis (or Hercules), the last of the shepherd kings, the five additional days <note>These are the "epagomenal" days added to the 360-day calendar to reach 365 days.</note> were placed in their year. And then, a solar year of 365 days first began among the Egyptians. It is somewhat odd that the Egyptians should call these five additional days by the word *Nesi*, which signifies a snake. I suppose they intended them to be sacred days, or holy days. They were placed at the end of the year and considered the birthdays of the gods. I suppose this came from some prior knowledge they had of the birth of the Messiah at that time of the year; for I find all antiquity had such notice.

But Syncellus does not tell us the whole truth: Abraham also taught Assis the intercalation of the quarter-day and the leap day every fourth year. For, according to what I have been able to find concerning this matter, the Mosaic or patriarchal year was solar and strictly "Julian" <note>The author argues that the 365.25-day calendar, later known as the Julian calendar, was actually used by the biblical patriarchs.</note> in form. But when the world was overwhelmed with idolatry, Providence judged it proper to alter the year as well, in order to break up <note>original: "dissicate"; likely a transcription error for "dissociate" or "dissipate"</note> their heathen and superstitious festivals. Therefore, God communicated to Moses the form of the lunar-solar year, which the Jews use to this day. But toward the coming of the Messiah, Providence took care to restore the ancient patriarchal year in the Julian form.

From this, we may account for what Herodotus tells us of the Thebans (a people in Upper Egypt) who added the quarter-day every fourth year from the earliest times—no doubt from the time of Hercules.

Let us mention this observation: In the sacred account of Abraham's journey here in Egypt, we find no distaste <note>original: "disaste"</note> from the Egyptians toward shepherds, which by the time of his grandson Jacob was an abomination to them. This...

<summary>This page argues that Abraham was a master astronomer who taught the Egyptians the 365.25-day solar year during his time in Egypt, identifying the Pharaoh of Genesis 12 as Melcarthus.</summary>

This *shows* that the *Shepherd* Kings <note>original: "pastor kings"</note> were reigning here at the time *Abraham* *conversed* with them. It also *shows* the *reason* for that *abomination* <note>The author refers to Genesis 46:34, which states that "every shepherd is an abomination unto the Egyptians."</note> felt toward shepherds when they were eventually *expelled*. This confirms *Manetho's* *history* of the *dynasties* and *illustrates* the *scriptures*. Since *Jacob's* family were *Canaanites* and *shepherds*, they were grouped with those who had held the *Egyptians* in such a long war. For this reason, *Joseph* *pretended* they were *spies* in *Genesis 42:9*.

Further, we have another very *important* piece of *history* resulting from *Abraham's* time in *Egypt*, which many *learned* people are not *aware* of. From this, it is more *than* a mere *presumption* that the *Egyptians* *learned* the *use* of *letters* or *alphabetical* *writing* from him. If we *search* the *accounts* *transmitted* to *us* by *literature* *concerning* their *own* *origins*, *Philo* the *Jew* *expressly* *attributes* the *invention* *of writing* to *Abraham*. Regarding this, *Plato* (in his dialogues *Philebus* and *Phaedrus*) *contends* that their *first* *appearance* in *Egypt* was *discovered* by *Theuth* <note>The Egyptian god of wisdom and writing, often identified with Hermes or Mercury.</note>. “Whether “ he was a *god* or a *man* is *doubtful*,” *Plato* *says*; *meaning* that the *use* of letters *must* have been a *divine* *communication*. *Syncellus* *writes* that “the *opinion* of *some* “is that *Abraham* *brought* *letters* out of *Chaldea* and *taught* them to “the *Phoenicians*, and they in turn *taught* them to the *Greeks*.” *Diodorus* (Book 5) *writes* that “the *Syrians* *invented* *letters*, and the *Phoenicians* *learned* the *great* *secret* “from them.” *Eusebius* (in his *Preparation for the Gospel*, Book 10) *confirms* this, but *asserts* “that by “the *Syrians* are *meant* the *Assyrians* (as was *often* the *case* in *old* *accounts*) “or *more* *specifically* the *Hebrews*.” In *truth*, it was the *ancestors* of *Abraham*. I believe this is the *real* *truth*: *God* *first* *imparted* this *knowledge* to the *patriarchal* *family* to *preserve* the *sacred* *records* of *his* *church*. *Abraham* then *taught* their *use* to *Assis*, the *Hercules* who was the *son* *of* the *Egyptian* *Jupiter* <note>original: "Nilus Jupiter"</note>, who *wrote* in *Phrygian* *letters*, according to *Cicero*.

All of this is *strongly* *confirmed* by the *explanation* *Mr. Toland* *gives* us *concerning* <term>Hercules Ogmius</term> <note>Ogmius was the Celtic god of eloquence, whom the Greeks identified with Hercules.</note> in his *History* of the *Druids*. *Lucian* noted that the word <term>Ogmius</term> had *previously* *been* *inexplicable*. He *describes* a *picture* of *him* <note>original: "in Hercule Gallico" (in the Gallic Hercules)</note> which he *saw* in *Gaul*, and which was *explained* to *him* by a *Druid*. *Hercules* was *pictured* *clad* in a *lion's* *skin*, holding a *club* in his *right* *hand* and a *bent* *bow* in his *left*, with a *quiver* *hanging* *over* his *shoulders*. As *for* his *physical form*, he was an *old* *man*, *bald* in front and *wrinkled*, looking like a *sun-burnt* *sailor*. A *multitude* of *people* were *represented* as being *drawn* after *him* by *golden* *chains* attached to their *ears*, which *centered* at his *tongue*. The *Druid* *told* *Lucian* that <term>Ogmius</term> *accomplished* his *great* *achievements* through his *eloquence*, and thereby *reduced* the *people* of this *western* *world* from a *rude* and *barbarous* *state* to a state of *civility*.

A *memorial* of this *knowledge* of *letters* which *Hercules* possessed is *found* in *Hephaestion* (Book 5), where he *writes* that “*Hercules* *gave* the *name* of *Alpha* to the “*first* *letter* in *honor* of the *river* *Alpheus* when he was *victor* at the *Olympic* “*games*.” *My* late *learned* *friend*, *Mr. Keysler*, in his *Antiquities of the North* <note>original: "Antiq. septentrional."</note>, *shows* *evidently* that <term>Ogum</term> <note>Ogham, an ancient British and Irish alphabetical script.</note> is a *word* in the *Irish* *language* *signifying* the *secret* of *alphabetical* *writing*—the *secrets of letters* <note>original: "literarum secreta"</note>, as *Tacitus* *calls* it in his book *On the Customs of the Germans* <note>original: "de mor. germ." (De Origine et situ Germanorum)</note>. So, <term>Hercules Ogmius</term> *fully* *implies* the *learned* *Hercules*, and *especially* one who was a *master* of *alphabetical* *writing*; *without* which, *learning* *is* but a *vague* and *uncertain* *thing*. This, our *Hercules*, *learned* from *Abraham* in the *East*, and he *brought* it with our *Druids* into the *farthest* *West* during this very *early* *age* of the *world*, as we have every *reason* *imaginable* to *believe*. That they possessed *letters*, we have *Julius* *Caesar's* *express* testimony,

I. A coin in <term>Vaillant’s</term> <note>original: Jean Foy-Vaillant, a 17th-century French numismatist</note> *Colonies*, Vol. II, pages 148, 218, 340, 351. From the city of *Tyre*, it shows an olive tree with a snake between two stones, known as <term>ambrosial stones</term> <note>original: "petræ ambrosiæ"; these were sacred stones believed to have been infused with divine essence</note>. It also features an altar and a conch shell, representing the city of *Tyre*.

II. A coin in <term>Vaillant’s</term> *Colony Coins*, Vol. II, page 314, struck at *Ptolemais* (also known as *Acon*). It shows a large, rough stone altar without any moldings or carvings, standing between two serpents. There is a <term>Caduceus</term>, which in <term>hieroglyphic doctrine</term> is actually the *serpent-circle-wing-form* <note>original: "ophio-cyclo-pterygomorph"; a symbolic representation of the deity's power</note> atop a staff, signifying the power of the deity. These imperial colony coins were intended to preserve the memory of their ancient origins, and this one likely refers to the old <term>serpentine temple</term> involved in the founding of the city of *Acon* or *Ptolemais*.

III. A coin in <term>Vaillant’s</term> *Colonies*, Vol. II, page 111, struck at *Berytus*. These coins all relate to **Hercules** building <term>serpentine temples</term>. Regarding his construction of our **Druid temples** in general using these great stones, the two coins of **Gordian** mentioned in the *Stonehenge* book on page 50 provide further evidence. The <term>Ambrosial Stones</term> are a work of this kind, created when he began or assisted in building the city of *Tyre*. I conclude that he was a prolific builder of serpentine temples in particular, like those we have been describing, called <term>Dracontia</term> <note>temples laid out in the shape of a snake</note>. While I have no evidence for discovering exactly what he did of this sort in **Britain**, ancient history still contains enough traces to show he did so in the more eastern parts of the world.

For instance, at *Acon* (later called *Ptolemais*), a city on the **Phoenician** shore: it has regained its original name and is now called **St. John of Acre**, named after a famous church there. The first city was probably built by our **Hercules**, or at least he built one of these temples there, as I infer from the name of the place, its coins, and the reports related to it. The **Greeks** call it *Ake*, and following their custom, they give it a Greek origin from the word <term>to be healed</term> <note>original: "ακεωθαι" (akeothai)</note>. This is because, says the *Great Etymological Dictionary* <note>original: "Etymologicum magnum"</note>, **Hercules** was healed there from the bite of a serpent. **Stephanus of Byzantium** agrees with this in his entry for *Ptolemais* (under the word *Ake*); he says that **Claudius Julius**, in the first volume of his *Phoenician History*, writes:

> "that the city received its name from **Hercules**, who was ordered by the oracle to travel eastward until he came to a river and found the herb <term>Colocasia</term>, which would cure his wound. He arrived at the river **Belus**, which flows into the sea here, and there he found the herb."

**Salmasius**, in his *Plinian Exercises*, confirms that the herb is <term>Dracunculus</term>. It grows in our gardens and is called **Dragons** because it looks like a snake’s head and tongue and is spotted like a snake. 

I can understand all of this in no other way than that **Hercules** built a serpentine temple on the bank of this river, where the city of *Acon* was later built. The city took its name from this temple, just as our **Hakpen** at **Abury** <note>Avebury in Wiltshire</note> did; for...

# ABURY

...for <term>אגן Acan</term> <note>original: "Acan" in Chaldee (an ancient Semitic language)</note> signifies a serpent, as we observed before. **Josephus** informs us that by the river **Belus** was the sepulcher of **Memnon**, which was probably located here out of respect for the temple.

When we come to **Greece**, we hear of **Hercules** overcoming the **Lernean snake**, which *Heraclides Ponticus* writes had 50 heads. We may very well understand this as 50 stones which composed the head of a temple, just as our temple on **Overton Hill** was composed of 58 stones. **Hephaestion II** recounts from *Alexander the Myndian* that this *Hydra* was turned into stone. In this way, hints and reports are dropped which preserve the real truth enveloped in fable, as was the **Greek** method in all matters of antiquity.

This snake was of an unusual size and lay near a great body of water called the **Lernean Lake**, by a large plane tree and the spring **Anymone**. Furthermore, it is said that in overcoming this animal (by which they mean the labor he bestowed in accomplishing the work), he used the help of **Iolaus** the wagoner. Such help must have been highly useful to him for transporting the **stones**. But I observe from the name of **Iolaus** his wagoner and companion, and **Hylas** another great friend of his, and **Iole** his mistress, that the ancient Druidical festival is hidden under that name, called **Yule**, which I shall speak extensively upon in its proper place. 

In the meantime, we are told the snake was afflicted against him by a very great crab. This will appear strange until we are directed to its meaning by this consideration: just as the serpent represents the **Dracontian** <note>serpentine</note> temple, so the crab was a symbol similar in figure and meaning to the *globus alatus* <note>original: "globus alatus"; winged circle</note> or winged circle, which was the ancient representation of the *anima mundi* <note>original: "anima mundi"; the world soul or divine spirit</note>. Thus mythology, when rightly considered, helps us in these ancient inquiries. We may say of the work as **Statius** does of the temple of *Hercules Surrentinus*:

->"The god, striving, moved the resisting rocks, and with a great heart pushed back the mountain." <note>original: "Deus obluctantia saxa / Summovit nitens, & magno pectore montem / Repulit."</note><-

There are similar vestiges of other **Dracontian** temples founded by **Hercules** in **Spain**, **Africa**, and elsewhere. 
"**Hercules**," says Bishop **Cumberland**, "was a very learned prince, educated or conversant in the **Phoenician** universities, of which **Debir** was one (**Joshua 15:15, 49**), called for its eminence **Kirjath-sepher** (the city of books) and **Kirjath-sanna** (the city of learning)." The bishop thinks he retreated from **Egypt** around the time of **Abraham’s** death. However, based on the chronological evidence I gave before, it must have been a good while before then. And I do not doubt that he with pleasure renewed his acquaintance with his old friend **Abraham** in the land of **Canaan**.

There seems to be very strong proof of this in the fact that **Hercules** had a son called **Isaac**, for whom one would imagine **Abraham** was a sponsor at his baptism—or perhaps his son **Isaac** was—for baptism was one part of the patriarchal religion. And they had "susceptors," sponsors, or what we call **godparents** at the font, just as we do today. Of this **Isaac**, son of **Hercules**, **Plutarch** informs us in his work *On Isis and Osiris* <note>original: "de Isid. & Osir."</note> that he was remembered by the **Phrygians**, for he was settled in **Phrygia** by his father **Hercules**. Hence it became a common name there, and **Aesacus**, son of King **Priam**, is simply the same name, as my learned friend **Mr. Baxter** thinks in his *Glossary of Roman Antiquities* <note>original: "glossar. Antiq. Rom."</note>. If this consideration is joined to what I wrote in *Stonehenge* about **Phryxus** (or **Apher**), the grandson of **Abraham**, having a role in settling and even naming **Britain**, it may...

## DESCRIBED.

This may provide us with another hint about our *Phrygian origin* <term>Phrygian extract: the belief that the ancient Britons were descended from the Trojans of Phrygia</term>, which the old Britons are so fond of. We can expect nothing more than these kinds of hints in matters of such extreme antiquity. Furthermore, as he was concerned with settling colonies in *Spain*, we may attribute to him the claim that the *Gallaeci* <note>original: Gallæci; a people of northwest Spain</note> there had to a *Trojan descent*, which the historian *Justin* informs us of. This *Apher* is the *Africus* mentioned by *Pomponius Mela* (Book I, Chapter 9). He calls him an *Arabian* king who, after being driven out by the *Assyrians*, went into *Africa*. It is very remarkable that his name, when interpreted, signifies *Tin*; just as the great scholar *Bochart* argues that the name of *Britain* comes from *Bratanac-tin*, which is equivalent to the Greek word for the *Tin Islands* <note>original: καττεριδες (kassiterides)</note>, from which we get the name *Cassiterides*. This expulsion seems to be hinted at in *Genesis 14:6* during the days of *Abraham*. Now, a reader unfamiliar with this type of research might smile at the attempt to find a similarity between *Apher* and *Britain*. The same might happen when deriving the *Wiltshire* word *sarsens* <note>Sarsen stones are the large sandstone blocks used to build Stonehenge and Avebury</note> from the same word as the name of the city of *Tyre*; though it is an undeniable fact and easily perceived by the learned.

The evidence that *Hercules* settled *Britain* is of a similar nature, which I shall briefly summarize. *Apollodorus* (in Book II), after the story of *Hercules*, *Antaeus*, and *Geryon* (two kings in *Africa* and *Spain*), mentions him conquering *Alebion* and *Dercynus*, the sons of *Neptune*. He tells this in the same mythological style as the other stories, claiming they were defeated because they attempted to drive away his cattle. He places this event in *Libya*, while others place it in *Ligya* or *Liguria*, and others in *Gaul*. This is of no consequence in these very old stories. I regard only the personal names of *Albion* and *Bergion*, as they are more commonly called—the sons of *Neptune*. If this is indeed the case, they are the sons of *Tarshish* <note>Tarshish was a grandson of Noah associated with maritime trade</note>, for *Tarshish* was the true *Neptune* of the pagans; and he was one of the sons to whom the pagans generally attribute the settlement of islands, as does *Moses* in *Genesis 10:5*. But *Albion* and *Bergion* are notoriously the most ancient names for *Britain* and *Ireland*. *Mela* (Book II, Chapter 5) mentions *Hercules* fighting *Albion* and *Bergion*. So do *Tzetzes* in his *Book of Thousands* <note>original: chiliad</note> and *Tzetzes* the interpreter of *Lycophron*.

*Tacitus* says expressly that *Hercules* was in *Germany*, especially in the part lying upon the ocean. *Ammianus Marcellinus*, in his Book XV, Chapter 9, tells us from the ancient historian *Timagenes* "that the *Dorians* <note>original: Dorienses</note>, following the more ancient *Hercules*, inhabited the western countries bordering on the ocean." Near Mount *Carmel* was a city called *Dora*, spoken of by *Josephus* and by *Stephanus of Byzantium*, who quoted *Hecataeus* and many more ancient authors. See the famous fragment of *Stephanus*. *Claudius Julius*, in his third book of *Phoenician* history, writes, "next to *Caesarea* is *Dora*, inhabited by *Phoenicians* because of the great quantity of the purple fish <note>Murex sea snails used to make dye</note> found there." Now, since *Hercules* is the confessed inventor of this *Tyrian dye* <term>Tyrian dye: a highly prized purple-red dye from the city of Tyre</term>, it is probable that his companions, mentioned by *Ammianus*, were from this city. If *Hercules* peopled the ocean coasts of *Gaul*, *Spain*, and *Germany*, we may well imagine he would do the like in *Britain*. *Pliny’s* testimony is express: that *Melqart* <note>original: Melcarthus; the Phoenician Hercules</note> (incorrectly written as *Midacritus* <note>original: Midaeritms</note>) first brought *tin* from the *Cassiterid islands*, which can be no other than *Britain*.

The poets and mythologists, when speaking of the *Titans*, agree they all went into the west, which seems to mean *Hercules* and his people settling in *Britain*. Our *Thule*, or northern island, seems to have been named by our *Hercules*—as a proof of his being there—after an island of the same name in the *Persian Gulf*. *Bochart* mentions this. The same is to be inferred from stories such as that related by *Parthenius*

# AVEBURY <note>original: ABURY; Stukeley’s spelling of the prehistoric site in Wiltshire</note>

Parthenius of Nicaea says <note>original: Nicaus</note> "that *Hercules*, while traveling after his expedition against *Geryon*, passed through the country of the *Celts* and was entertained by *Britannus*. His daughter *Celtine* fell in love with him, and by him he fathered a son called *Celtus*; from him, the Celtic people afterwards received their name."

We noted previously that the shepherds who left *Egypt* under the leadership of our *Hercules* called themselves *Hyssi*, as Manetho <note>original: Manethon; an Egyptian priest and historian</note> informs us in the works of *Josephus* and *Eusebius* in his *Chronicon* <note>original: chronol.</note>. The word signifies "royal *shepherds*," who were valiant *freemen* and *heroes*. Now, we find the remains of this very name in the southwestern part of our island, in *Worcestershire*, surviving even to *Roman* times and further still, to the time of the Venerable *Bede*. They were called *Hwiccii* <note>original: Huiccii; an ancient Anglo-Saxon tribe</note>, which is synonymous with the names *Ordovices* and *Vigornienses*. All three words mean the same thing, as the great scholar William *Baxter* shows in his glossary, *Ancient British Antiquities* <note>original: Antiq. Britan.</note>, under the entries <note>original: voce</note> for *Ordovices*, *Iceni*, *Hwiccii*, and others. By all accounts, our ancient *Britons* loved that same free, shepherd’s life that the ancient *Canaanites* lived during *Abraham’s* time, as described in the Bible. Bishop *Cumberland* writes about this in great detail.

I consider the *Irish* and the ancient highland *Scots* to be the descendants of the original *Phoenician* colony. My learned friend, Dr. *Pococke*, when he was in *Ireland*, observed a surprising similarity between the modern *Irish* and the *Egyptians* in a great many instances.

These considerations, added to what I said in my work on *Stonehenge*, are enough to persuade us that our *Hercules* had a significant hand in the settling of *Britain*.

## CHAPTER XIV.

Part of the history of <term>Cadmus</term>, who was a builder of <term>serpentine temples</term> <note>temples built in the shape of a snake, such as Avebury</note>. He was the son of <term>Canaan</term>, whom the Greeks called <term>Agenor</term>. He was a <term>Horite</term> or <term>Hivite</term>, called a <term>Kadmonite</term> in scripture. <term>Hivite</term> signifies a <term>serpent</term>. <term>Mount Hermon</term> was named after his wife; *Psalm 133:3* says, "as the dew of <term>Hermon</term>, which fell on the hill of <term>Sion</term>." This should be corrected to *<term>Sirion</term>* <note>Sirion is the Phoenician name for Mount Hermon; the author argues the Psalm refers to the same mountain range rather than Jerusalem's Mount Zion</note>. Another correction in the translation of our Bible: where it says "there shall be no more the <term>Canaanite</term> in the house of the Lord of hosts," it should read "merchant." This is a prophecy often overlooked in *<term>Zechariah 14:21</term>*. The ancient <term>Greek fables</term> of sowing <term>serpents</term>' teeth, and of <term>Cadmus</term> and his wife being turned into <term>serpents</term> and the like, were created because of their practice of building <term>serpentine temples</term>. This is not so surprising when we consider that our own country people have the very same reports about the <term>Rollright Stones</term>; about <term>The Weddings</term> (another <term>Druid temple in Somersetshire</term>); and about <term>Long Meg and her daughters</term> (another in <term>Cumberland</term>). Local people firmly believe these stones were once men and women turned into stone. The mythology of the ancients should not be dismissed, but its original meaning must be sought out.

No one is more famous in <term>Grecian history</term> than <term>Cadmus</term>, who brought them the use of the letters that conveyed their history to us and preserved the little knowledge we have of secular antiquity. He was the son of <term>Agenor</term>, which was the name the <term>Greeks</term> chose to use for the difficult name of <term>Canaan</term>. <term>Alexander Polyhistor</term> cites from *<term>Eupolemus</term>*: "from <term>Saturn</term> (who is <term>Ham</term>) came <term>Belus</term> and <term>Canaan</term>, and <term>Canaan</term> fathered the father of the <term>Phoenicians</term>, or <term>Phoenix</term>." *<term>Eusebius</term>* <note>original: "Eusebius, pr. ev. 9." referring to his work "Preparation for the Gospel," book 9</note> also records this. Again, *<term>Eusebius</term>* quotes from *<term>Sanchoniathon</term>* that "<term>Cna</term> (meaning <term>Canaan</term>) was titled among the <term>Phoenicians</term> as <term>Chna</term> <note>original Greek: "XHNA"</note>." Likewise, in the works of *<term>Stephanus of Byzantium</term>*, <term>Phoenicia</term> is called <term>Chna</term> <note>original Greek: "XHNA"</note>, and the <term>Phoenicians</term> are called <term>Chnai</term> <note>original Greek: "XHNAI"</note>, which means <term>Canaanites</term>. <term>Chna</term> is the same as <term>Agenor</term>.

<term>Cadmus</term> lived at the same time as, or very shortly after, <term>Hercules</term>. Although the <term>Parian Marble</term> <note>an ancient Greek chronological table carved into stone</note> is an invaluable monument, it is not an infallible one. If the learned scholar <term>Bentley</term> found it contained errors regarding the poet <term>Stesichorus</term>, we must not depend entirely on its date for <term>Cadmus</term>, who lived a thousand years before that stone was even made. Nor is the authority of *<term>Eusebius's</term>* chronology in this particular case any greater. The scholar <term>Bochart</term> believes he is older than the builder of <term>Tyre</term>, though perhaps he pushes his date back a little too far.

To have a proper understanding of the history of this great man, Bishop <term>Cumberland</term> shows us that the <term>Horites</term> or <term>Hivites</term> (sons of <term>Canaan</term>, meaning the colony or people of <term>Cadmus</term>, son of <term>Agenor</term> or <term>Canaan</term>) left the land of <term>Canaan</term> at about the same time that <term>Mizraim</term> or <term>Osiris</term> (the son of <term>Ham</term>) went to settle <term>Egypt</term>. They also went into <term>Egypt</term> and lived quietly there...

<summary>This page discusses the historical and mythological identity of Cadmus, identifying him as a Canaanite/Hivite who built snake-shaped temples. The author connects Greek myths and biblical genealogy to explain the origins of ancient stone monuments in both Greece and Britain.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's argument linking ancient Mediterranean mythology and Phoenician history to the construction of "serpentine" temples, drawing parallels between these sites and British monuments like Avebury and Stonehenge.</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>

-># AVEBURY <-

It was from the city of Sarepta that Europa was carried off; it is in the country of Sidon. I believe, based on its name, that there was originally a <term>serpentine temple</term> <note>A temple laid out in the shape of a snake, which the author calls a "Dracontium"</note> there. Sarepta means the "serpent Ptha" <note>The author likely refers to the Egyptian creator god, Ptah</note>. I have an ancient brass coin from this city: a palm tree on one side and a leopard’s face on the other, which refers to the famous wine of the region, mentioned by the learned Reland in *Palestine*.

Conon, in his 37th narration, gives us the origin of the <term>Greek fable</term> of Cadmus’s men—the Phoenicians—who were said to spring out of the ground fully armed. This was because, before that time, helmets and shields were unknown in that region. Therefore, they were called *Spartae* <note>meaning "the Sown Men"</note>. The story that these armed men sprang out of the ground upon the sowing of the serpent’s teeth refers to our Hivites <note>A biblical people whom the author identifies as ancient serpent-worshipers</note> making a <term>religious procession</term> along the avenues of their <term>serpentine temples</term> on the great festival days when they performed sacrifices. We see a similar procession of armed men carved upon the temple of Persepolis in Cornelis de Bruyn’s prints. Ovid also calls a Boeotian (one of Cadmus’s people) *Hyantius* (*Iliad* 5, verse 147). Strabo (Book 7) writes that they took that name from their king Hyas, which is the same as "Hivite." Pliny (Book 4, chapter 7) observes that the Boeotians were called by this name in ancient times.

In the next book (*Metamorphoses* Book 4, verse 560), we have an account of Melicerta (our Melqart <note>A Phoenician god often identified with Hercules</note>) and his mother being deified. We also hear of the Sidonian women, their companions, who were turned some into stones and others into birds for grieving at their fate. This seems to refer to the practice of building temples—using the methods we have been describing and those to follow in Chapter 16—near the graves of heroes and founders of states, as was the ancient custom. This is similar to what we observed at Silbury Hill and Avebury. These temples were protective and served as a sacred guard for the ashes of the deceased. We read of this in Virgil, regarding the tomb of Anchises in the *Aeneid*, Book 5:

->Then a seat near the stars on the Erycinian peak<-
->Is founded for Idalian Venus; and for the tomb, a priest<-
->And a grove, sacred far and wide, are added for Anchises.<-
<note>original: "Tunc vicina astris Erycino in vertice sedes / Fundatur Veneri Idaliæ; tumuloque sacerdos / Ac lucus latè sacer additur Anchisæo."</note>

Immediately after Ovid’s account of Melicerta, the poet speaks of Cadmus and his wife being turned into serpents. I understand this to mean that a similar serpentine temple was built by their burial place. Suidas writes that on the tomb of Epaminondas, a shield and a snake were carved to show he was of the "Spartan" (Sown) race. We may well imagine that the circle and snake were the heraldic symbol of Cadmus.

After Cadmus’s death, his people built a city called Butua. Near it is a place called Cylices, where Cadmus and Harmonia were turned into serpents; two stone snakes were set up there by the Phoenicians in their honor. This is noted by Bochart on page 502, where many authors are quoted to prove these details. He says the word *Cylices* in the Phoenician language means "burial mounds"—our <term>barrows</term>. It was a place full of burial mounds, just like Stonehenge and Avebury; these mounds look like overturned cups in their shape. Nonnus, in his *Dionysiaca*, writes that there are two great stones or rocks there that clap together with a loud noise, from which omens are taken. Tzetzes (*Chiliades* 4, history 139) mentions the same thing. I take this to be a "main <term>ambre</term>," <note>A "Main Amber" refers to a balanced or "rocking" stone, which antiquarians believed were sacred to the Druids</note> which I discussed at length in my work on Stonehenge. Herodotus (Book 5, chapter 61) says that when the Cadmeans were admitted as citizens of Athens, they built temples there that had nothing in common with Greek temples; specifically, they had a temple of *Ceres Achæa* and mystical rites. I suppose *Achæa* means a serpentine temple, derived from its Oriental name.

<summary>The author connects Mediterranean myths of Cadmus and the Phoenicians to the construction of serpentine temples and burial mounds, suggesting that sites like Avebury share a common architectural and religious origin with these ancient Eastern traditions.</summary>
<keywords>Sarepta, Cadmus, Phoenicians, Hivites, serpentine temples, Dracontia, barrows, burial mounds, Avebury, Melqart, mythology, ancient architecture</keywords>
