<meta>The author continues describing the Druidic deity Taramis, equating him with other ancient gods like Thoth and Mercury, and explores the ancient symbolism of the cross across various cultures before shifting focus to the physical grandeur of the temple at Abury (Avebury).</meta>

<page-num>101</page-num>
<header>DESCRIBED.</header>

Taramis is the same as *Tat* or *Thoth* of the *Egyptians*, and *Thor* of the northern nations, called more particularly the spirit: lord of the air, from the *Phoenician* and *Celtic Tarem*. He was sometimes called *Theutates*, the *Mercury* of the *Latins*, who was particularly worshipped by the *Germans*, says Tacitus in *On the Customs of the Germans* <note>original: "de mor. germ."</note>. Caesar says the same in *The Gallic War* <note>original: "VI. bell. gall."</note>, Book 6. Because of this, the *Greeks* depicted their *Mercury* with a winged cap and winged heels, which was nothing other than the *winged circle* <note>original: "circulus alatus"</note> we have been speaking of. He bears a staff in his hand—a globe on the end of it with wings and snakes <note>The caduceus, the traditional staff of Hermes/Mercury</note>. The *Phoenicians* called him *Taautus*. See Sanchoniathon and Varro, *On the Latin Language* <note>original: "IV. de ling. lat."</note>, Book 4.

In the temple of *Belus* (the *sun*) at *Edessa* in *Mesopotamia*, during idolatrous times, there was a statue of him next to another of *Ezizus*, who is our *Hesus*, and another of *Mercury*, whom they called *Monimus*. *Julian* <note>Julian the Apostate, the Roman Emperor</note>, in his hymn to the *sun*, mentions the same. And so, generally, the true theology communicated to mankind from the beginning was perverted into polytheism and idolatry.

3. Thus, as death came by the tree, life came by the tree—a concept the *Druids* seem to have had some knowledge of. Rufinus <note>original: "Ruffinus II. 29."</note> affirms the cross among the *Egyptians* was a hieroglyphic signifying "the life that is to come." *Sozomen* says the same in *Ecclesiastical History* <note>original: "hist. eccl. VII. 15."</note>, as does *Suidas*. Isidore tells us, “it was the method of the muster-masters in the *Roman* army, when giving in the lists of the soldiers, to mark with a cross the name of the man who was alive, and with a ☉ <note>This symbol, often representing the sun, was sometimes used similarly to the Greek letter theta (Θ) to mark the deceased (thanatos)</note> the one who was dead.”

The ancient inhabitants of *America* honored the form of the cross. So, too, do the conjurers in *Lapland* use it. This suggests this hieroglyphic is most ancient, probably antediluvian <term>from before the biblical Great Flood</term>.

But concerning the knowledge of the cross which the *Druids* had, and of their religion more broadly, I shall discourse fully in the next volume, which will conclude what I have to say concerning them and their works.

4. From what has been presented in the speculative part of this treatise, the origins of idolatry appear clearly enough. For the race of heroes that built these patriarchal temples in the eastern part of the world especially, and propagated true religion, were some ages later deified by their idolatrous descendants. They were given names of consecration taken from divine attributes and the correct notions delivered to them concerning the nature of the deity.

5. If then we reflect on the previous description of the work at *Abury* <note>Modern-day Avebury in Wiltshire</note>, whether we consider the figure it is built upon, its antiquity, or its grandeur, we must admire it as deservedly ranked among the greatest wonders on the face of the earth. The ancients indeed made huge temples of immense pillars in colonnades, like a small forest, or vast concave domes to represent the heavens; they made gigantic statues to represent their gods. But to our *British Druids* was reserved the honor of a more extensive idea and of executing it. They have made plains and hills, valleys, springs, and rivers contribute to form a temple three miles in length. They have stamped a whole country with the impression of this sacred character, and one of the most permanent nature. The golden temple of *Solomon* has vanished; the proud structure of the *Babylonian Belus*, the temple of *Diana* at *Ephesus*, that of *Vulcan* in *Egypt*, and that of the *Capitoline Jupiter* are perished and obliterated. Meanwhile *Abury*—which I dare say was older than any of them—was entire only a very few years ago at the beginning of this century. Even now, there are sufficient traces left to provide a perfect understanding of the whole. Since I began visiting the place, I fear it has suffered damage; but at that time, there was scarce a single stone in the ori—

<meta>catchword: ginal</meta>

<summary>This page equates various ancient deities with the Druidic Taramis and Hesus, argues for the ancient and universal symbolism of the cross, and extols the architectural genius of the Druids at Abury, comparing it favorably to the lost wonders of the ancient world.</summary>

--- Page 220 ---
<meta>The author continues their exploration of ancient British religious sites, arguing that the Druidic temples were part of a "patriarchal" religion that predated formal Judaism and aligned with the core tenets of Christianity.</meta>

<page-num>102</page-num>
<header>A B U R Y, etc.</header>

[The ori]ginal ground plan <note>original: "ground-plot"</note> was missing, but I could trace its history back to the person then living who demolished it, as well as for what purpose and where the materials were used.

I truly believe this was a genuine patriarchal temple, just like the others we have described here, where the worship of the true God was performed. And I conclude with what *Epiphanius* <note>A 4th-century church father known for his writings on heresies and the history of the early church.</note> writes when speaking of the ancient religion from the beginning of the world: "It was not Judaism or any other sect; but if I may say so, it was the same faith which the holy Catholic Church of God now holds; which, though it existed from the beginning, was afterwards manifested again" <note>original: "Non erat judaismus aut secta quæpiam alia: sed ut ita dicam, ea quæ nunc in præsenti sancta Dei catholica ecclesia obtinet, fides erat; quæ cum ab initio extiterit, postea rursum est manifestata."</note>. He affirms that *Adam* and all the patriarchs from him until *Abraham* were nothing other than Christians; and this is the doctrine of the Apostle to the Gentiles <note>Saint Paul</note>, 1 *Corinthians* 9:21.

<meta>catchword: INDEX.</meta>

<summary>The author argues that ancient stone temples like Avebury were sites of "patriarchal" worship dedicated to the true God, asserting that this original faith predated Judaism and was essentially an early form of Christianity.</summary>
